\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}}

if and only if
t\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}}

and
\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}}
.

2.

\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}}
if and only if

t\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}}

and
\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}}
.

3.

\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}}
if and only if

t\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal{A}^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}},\mathcal{S}^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}}}

and
\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}}
.

Lemma D.13 ((New) Invariants for the GLAMoUr).

Let
s_{r}
be a reachable state from an initial state

(\epsilon,\mathbf{t}_{0},\epsilon,\epsilon)
.
Then,
s_{r}
verifies the following invariant:

1.

\{\!\!\{s_{r}\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}

2.

If
s_{r}=(D,\mathbf{t},\pi_{1}:\phi^{l}:\pi_{2},E)
,
then
\phi^{l}
is
E
-rigid.

3.

If
s_{r}=(D,\mathbf{t},\pi,E_{1}:[x\backslash\phi^{l}]:E_{2})
,
then
\phi^{l}
is
E_{2}
-rigid.

Proof.

We prove that each transition step
\leadsto
in the GLAMoUr machine
preserves the invariant
i.e.
, if
s\leadsto s^{\prime}
and
s
verifies the invariant,
then
s^{\prime}
verifies the invariant as well.
We proceed by induction on the transition step
\leadsto
.

•

If
s=(D,\mathbf{t}\,\mathbf{s},\pi,E)\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{1}}(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),\mathbf{s},\epsilon,E)=s^{\prime}

then

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t}\,\mathbf{s},\pi,E)\}\!\!\}&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\,s\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\,s\rangle\rangle\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle\rangle)\langle s\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle(\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle)\langle\Diamond\rangle\rangle)\langle s\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle(\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle)\langle\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle s\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D:(\mathbf{t},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle s\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),\mathbf{s},\epsilon,E)\}\!\!\}\end{array}

Therefore
\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
holds
as the translation of both states is the same.
Item 2 of the invariant trivially holds
for
s^{\prime}
since it has an empty stack,
and item 3 holds for
s^{\prime}

since it holds for
s
which has the same environment
E
.

•

If
s=(D,\lambda x.\,\mathbf{t},\phi^{l}:\pi,E)\leadsto_{{\mathtt{u}}{\mathtt{m}}}(D,\mathbf{t},\pi,[x\backslash\phi^{l}]:E)=s^{\prime}
,
then
s
verifies the invariant:

1’.

\{\!\!\{(D,\lambda x.\,\mathbf{t},\phi^{l}:\pi,E)\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle(\lambda x.\,t)\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\rangle\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
.
Then by
Remark

D.12

\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}
,
\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}
and
\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}
are all in

\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}},\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}}}
,
and
\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}

2’.

\phi^{l}
is
E
-rigid, and
if
\pi
is of the form
\pi_{1}:\psi^{l}:\pi_{2}
,
then
\psi^{l}
is
E
-rigid

3’.

If
E
is of the form
E_{1}:[y\backslash\psi^{l}]:E_{2}
,
then
\psi^{l}
is
E_{2}
-rigid.

Let us show that the invariant holds for
s^{\prime}
:

1.

\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t[x\backslash\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}]\rangle\rangle\rangle\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
:
this holds directly from (1’).

2.

If
\pi
is of the form
\pi_{1}:\psi^{l}:\pi_{2}
,
then
\psi^{l}
is
E
-rigid:
this holds directly from (2’).

3.

\phi^{l}
is
E
-rigid, by item (2’), and the rest of this condition holds by (3’).

•

If
s=(D,x,\phi^{l}:\pi,E)\leadsto_{{\mathtt{u}}{\mathtt{e}}}(D,\mathbf{t}^{\alpha},\phi^{l}:\pi,E)=s^{\prime}
,
where
E=E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2}
,
then
s
verifies the invariant:

1’.

\{\!\!\{(D,x,\phi^{l}:\pi,E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2})\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle x\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
.
Then by
Remark

D.12

\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}
,
\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}
and
\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}
are all in

\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}},\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}}}
,
and
\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2}\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}

2’.

\phi^{l}
is
E
-rigid

3’.

\mathbf{t}^{\mathbb{A}}
is
E_{2}
-rigid

Let us show that the invariant holds for
s^{\prime}
:

1.

\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
: this holds directly from (1’)

2.

\phi^{l}
is
E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{t}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2}
-rigid:
this holds directly from (2’)

3.

\mathbf{t}^{\mathbb{A}}
is
E_{2}
-rigid:
this holds directly from (3’).

•

If
s=(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),\lambda x.\,\mathbf{s},\epsilon,E)\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{2}}(D,\mathbf{t},(\lambda x.\,\mathbf{s})^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi,E)=s^{\prime}

then

\displaystyle\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),\lambda x.\,\mathbf{s},\epsilon,E)\}\!\!\}

\displaystyle=(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D:(\mathbf{t},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle\lambda x.\,s\rangle

\displaystyle=(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle\langle\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle\lambda x.\,s\rangle

\displaystyle=(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle\langle\Diamond\rangle\rangle)\langle\lambda x.\,s\rangle

\displaystyle=(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle\rangle)\langle\lambda x.\,s\rangle

\displaystyle=(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\,\lambda x.\,s\rangle\rangle\rangle)

\displaystyle=(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond{(\lambda x.\,s)}\rangle\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle

\displaystyle=(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{(\lambda x.\,\mathbf{s})^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle

\displaystyle=\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t},(\lambda x.\,\mathbf{s})^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi,E)\}\!\!\}

Therefore
\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
holds
as the translation of both states is the same.
Item 2 of the invariant trivially holds
for
s^{\prime}
since
\lambda x.\,s\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}

for any abstraction frame
\mathcal{A}
.
Item 3 holds for
s^{\prime}
since it holds for
s
which has the same environment
E
.

•

If

s=(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\pi^{\prime},E)\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{3}}(D,\mathbf{t},(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi,E)=s^{\prime}
,
with
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(E)
, then

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\pi^{\prime},E)\}\!\!\}&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D:(\mathbf{t},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle\langle\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\,\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\,\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle x\rangle\rangle\rangle\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond{\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle x\rangle}\rangle\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond{\{\!\!\{(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}\}\!\!\}}\rangle\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t},(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi,E)\}\!\!\}\end{array}

Therefore
\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
holds
as the translation of both states is the same.
Item 2 of the invariant holds for
s^{\prime}

since
\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle x\rangle\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}}}
,
given the fact that
x\in\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})
.
And item 3 holds for
s^{\prime}
since it holds for
s
which has the same environment
E
.

•

If
s=(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\pi^{\prime},E_{1}:[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]:E_{2})\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{4}}(D,\mathbf{t},(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi,E_{1}:[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]:E_{2})=s^{\prime}

then

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\pi^{\prime},E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D:(\mathbf{t},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&((\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle\{\!\!\{D:(\mathbf{t},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D:(\mathbf{t},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle\langle\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\,\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\,\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle x\rangle\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle x\rangle\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle})\langle t\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle})\langle t\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle})\langle t\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{{E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]E_{2}}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t},(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi,E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}\end{array}

Therefore
\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
holds
as the translation of both states is the same.
Item 2 of the invariant holds for
s^{\prime}

since
\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle x\rangle\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})^{\{\!\!\{E_{1}:[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]:E_{2}\}\!\!\}}}
,
since the stack item is decorated with the label
\mathbb{S}
.
And item 3 holds for
s^{\prime}
since it holds for
s
which has the same environment
E
.

•

If

s=(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\epsilon,E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2})\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{5}}(D,\mathbf{t},x^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi,E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2})=s^{\prime}

then

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\epsilon,E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D:(\mathbf{t},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle\langle\{\!\!\{D:(\mathbf{t},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D:(\mathbf{t},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle\langle\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle\langle\Diamond\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\Diamond\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\,x\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond x\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle})\langle t\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{x^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle})\langle t\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{x^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2}}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{x^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t},x^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi,E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}\end{array}

Therefore
\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
holds
as the translation of both states is the same.
Item 2 of the invariant holds since

x\in\mathsf{HA}_{\varnothing^{\{\!\!\{E_{1}:E_{2}\}\!\!\}}\cup\{x\}}
,
since the code
\mathbf{s}
is decorated with the label
\mathbb{A}
.
And item 3 for
s^{\prime}
holds since it holds for
s
which has the same environment
E
.

∎

Lemma D.14.

Let
x\notin\mathsf{fv}(\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\})
and
x\notin\mathsf{fv}(\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\})
.
Then:

1.

\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t[x\backslash u]\rangle\equiv\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle[x\backslash u]

2.

\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle t[x\backslash u]\rangle\equiv\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle[x\backslash u]

Proof.

1.

We proceed by induction on the structure of
\pi
.

•

If
\pi=\epsilon
, then

\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\langle t[x\backslash u]\rangle=\Diamond\langle t[x\backslash u]\rangle=t[x\backslash u]\equiv\Diamond\langle t\rangle[x\backslash u]=\{\!\!\{\epsilon\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle[x\backslash u]
.

•

If
\pi=\phi^{l}:\pi^{\prime}
, then
by
\alpha
-equivalence, we may assume
in particular
x\notin\mathsf{fv}(\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\})
.
Therefore

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}:\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle t[x\backslash u]\rangle&=&\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\langle t[x\backslash u]\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle t[x\backslash u]\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\\
&\equiv&\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle(t\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\})[x\backslash u]\rangle&\text{(by $\textup{{es-l-dist}}$, since $x\notin\mathsf{fv}(\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\})$)}\\
&\equiv&\{\!\!\{\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle t\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle[x\backslash u]&\text{(by {\it i.h.} on $\pi^{\prime}$)}\\
&=&\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}:\pi^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle[x\backslash u]\end{array}

2.

We proceed by induction on the structure of
D
.

•

If
D=\epsilon
, then it is analogous to the base case in the previous item.

•

If
D=D^{\prime}:(\mathbf{s},\pi)
, then
by
\alpha
-equivalence, we may assume in particular

x\notin\mathsf{fv}(s)
.
Therefore

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\{\!\!\{D^{\prime}:(\mathbf{s},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle t[x\backslash u]\rangle&=&\{\!\!\{D^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle s\,\Diamond\rangle\rangle\langle t[x\backslash u]\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{D^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle s\,t[x\backslash u]\rangle\rangle\\
&\equiv&\{\!\!\{D^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle(s\,t)[x\backslash u]\rangle\rangle&\text{(by $\textup{{es-r-dist}}$, since $x\notin\mathsf{fv}(s)$)}\\
&\equiv&\{\!\!\{D^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle s\,t\rangle[x\backslash u]\rangle&\text{(by (1))}\\
&\equiv&\{\!\!\{D^{\prime}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle s\,t\rangle\rangle[x\backslash u]&\text{(by {\it i.h.} on $D^{\prime}$)}\\
&=&\{\!\!\{D^{\prime}:(\mathbf{s},\pi)\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle[x\backslash u]\end{array}

∎

See
6.1

Proof.

We proceed by case analysis of
\leadsto
.
To lighten the proof, we simplify
the notation
[x\backslash\phi^{l}]:E

by
[x\backslash\phi^{l}]E
.

1.

If
(D,\lambda x.\,\mathbf{t},\phi^{l}:\pi,E)\leadsto_{{\mathtt{u}}{\mathtt{m}}}(D,\mathbf{t},\pi,[x\backslash\phi^{l}]:E)

then

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\{\!\!\{(D,\lambda x.\,\mathbf{t},\phi^{l}:\pi,E)\}\!\!\}&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle\lambda x.\,t\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\rangle)\langle\lambda x.\,t\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle(\lambda x.\,t)\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\rangle\\
&&\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}_{\mathsf{db},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(s_{0}),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}&(\star)\\
&&\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t[x\backslash\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}]\rangle\rangle\rangle\\
&\equiv&\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle\rangle[x\backslash\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}]\rangle&\text{By \lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{lem:equivalence_in_translations}}(*)\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle[x\backslash\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}]\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond[x\backslash\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}]\rangle\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{[x\backslash\phi^{l}]E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t},\pi,[x\backslash\phi^{l}]E)\}\!\!\}\end{array}

The step
(\star)
holds by the following:

\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle(\lambda x.\,t)\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\rangle\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}
and

\phi^{l}
is
E
-rigid by
Lemma

D.13
(items 1 and 2) respectively.
Then

\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\in\mathsf{HA}_{\varnothing^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}}}\cup\mathsf{St}_{\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}}}
,
so applying congruence reduction rules accordingly to reach the redex

(\lambda x.\,t)\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}
, we can reduce this subterm with
rule
db-stable
∙
, so that

(\lambda x.\,t)\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}_{\mathsf{db},\varnothing^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}},\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})^{\{\!\!\{E\}\!\!\}},\mu}t[x\backslash\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}]
.

On the other hand, the step
(*)
holds applying rule
es-l-dist
,
as
x\notin\mathsf{fv}(\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\})
and
x\notin\mathsf{fv}(\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\})

holds by
\alpha
-conversion.

2.

If
(D,x,\phi^{l}:\pi,E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2})\leadsto_{{\mathtt{u}}{\mathtt{e}}}(D,\mathbf{v}^{\alpha},\phi^{l}:\pi,E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2})

then

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\{\!\!\{(D,x,\phi^{l}:\pi,E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}&=&\{\!\!\{F_{(D,x,\phi^{l}:\pi,E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2})}\}\!\!\}\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&((\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle)\langle x\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle x\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle\\
&&\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}_{\mathsf{lsv},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0}),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}&(\star)\\
&&\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle t\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle})\langle t\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle})\langle t\rangle\\
&=&({\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\Diamond\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&(\{\!\!\{{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2}}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}:\pi\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle)\langle t\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{F_{(D,\mathbf{v}^{\alpha},\phi^{l}:\pi,E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2})}\}\!\!\}\langle t\rangle\\
&=&\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t}^{\alpha},\phi^{l}:\pi,E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}\end{array}

The step
(\star)
holds since

\{\!\!\{E_{2}\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{D\}\!\!\}\langle\{\!\!\{\pi\}\!\!\}\langle x\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\rangle\rangle\{\!\!\{E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{v}^{\mathbb{A}}]\}\!\!\}\rangle\in\mathsf{Stable}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})}

by item 1 of
Lemma

D.13
.
So applying congruence reduction
rules and rule
lsv
∙
accordingly to reach the redex

x\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}
, we can reduce this subterm with
rule
appL
∙
, so that the reduction

x\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\varnothing^{\{\!\!\{E_{1}E_{2}\}\!\!\}}\cup\{x\},\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})^{\{\!\!\{E_{1}E_{2}\}\!\!\}},\mu}v\,\{\!\!\{\phi^{l}\}\!\!\}
,
is derived from applying rule
sub
∙
:

x\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\varnothing^{\{\!\!\{E_{1}E_{2}\}\!\!\}}\cup\{x\},\mathsf{fv}(t_{0})^{\{\!\!\{E_{1}E_{2}\}\!\!\}},@}v
.

3.

If
(D,\mathbf{t}\,\mathbf{s},\pi,E)\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{1}}(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),\mathbf{s},\epsilon,E)

then
\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t}\,\mathbf{s},\pi,E)\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),\mathbf{s},\epsilon,E)\}\!\!\}
,
is proved as in case
\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{1}}
in
Lemma

D.13
.

4.

If
(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),\lambda x.\,\mathbf{s},\epsilon,E)\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{2}}(D,\mathbf{t},(\lambda x.\,\mathbf{s})^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi,E)

then

\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),\lambda x.\,\mathbf{s},\epsilon,E)\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t},(\lambda x.\,\mathbf{s})^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi,E)\}\!\!\}
,
is proved as in case
\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{2}}
in
Lemma

D.13
.

5.

If

(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\pi^{\prime},E)\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{3}}(D,\mathbf{t},(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi,E)
,
with
x\notin\mathtt{dom}(E)
, then

\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\pi^{\prime},E)\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t},(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi,E)\}\!\!\}
,
is proved as in case
\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{3}}
in
Lemma

D.13
.

6.

If
(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\pi^{\prime},E_{1}:[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]:E_{2})\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{4}}(D,\mathbf{t},(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi,E_{1}:[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]:E_{2})

then

\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\pi^{\prime},E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}=\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t},(x,\pi^{\prime})^{\mathbb{S}}:\pi,E_{1}[x\backslash\phi^{\mathbb{S}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}

is proved as in case
\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{4}}
in
Lemma

D.13
.

7.

If
(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\epsilon,E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2})\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{5}}(D,\mathbf{t},x^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi,E_{1}:[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]:E_{2})

then

\{\!\!\{(D:(\mathbf{t},\pi),x,\epsilon,E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}\ =\{\!\!\{(D,\mathbf{t},x^{\mathbb{A}}:\pi,E_{1}[x\backslash\mathbf{s}^{\mathbb{A}}]E_{2})\}\!\!\}

is proved as in case
\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{5}}
in
Lemma

D.13
.

∎

Now, we give the main results of this section.
On the first hand, we have the high-level implementation theorem:
See
6.3

Proof.

Let
t\to_{\beta_{f}}^{n}t^{\prime}
.
By Thm. 8, Thm. 9 and Coro. 1 in
[
Accattoli and Coen, 2015
]
,
there is a sequence of
p
transitions

s\leadsto^{p}s^{\prime}

in the GLAMoUr,
where
s
is an initial state such that
\{\!\!\{s\}\!\!\}=t

and
p\in O(|t|\cdot(n^{2}+1))
.
By
Lemmas

6.1
and
6.2

there exists a term
t^{\prime\prime}
such that

t=\{\!\!\{s\}\!\!\}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}^{\raisebox{-2.84544pt}{{\footnotesize{$k$}}}}t^{\prime\prime}\equiv\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}

where
k\leq p
, so
k\in O(|t|\cdot(n^{2}+1))
.
To conclude, we are left to show that
t^{\prime\prime\Downarrow}=t^{\prime}
.
It is easy to see that
t^{\prime\prime}\equiv\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}

implies
t^{\prime\prime\Downarrow}=\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}^{\Downarrow}
,
so it suffices to show that
\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}^{\Downarrow}=t^{\prime}
.
This is a consequence of Thm. 3, Def. 1, and Thm. 8 in
[
Accattoli and Coen, 2015
]
.
∎

Next we move on the low-level implementation theorem:
See
6.4

Proof.

First we claim that the GLAMoUr terminates when starting from the initial
state
s
.
Indeed,
\leadsto
can be written as the union of
\leadsto_{{\mathtt{u}}{\mathtt{m}},{\mathtt{u}}{\mathtt{e}}}

and
\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{1},..,{\mathtt{c}}_{5}}
.
The relation
\leadsto_{{\mathtt{c}}_{1},..,{\mathtt{c}}_{5}}
is known to be terminating
from
[
Accattoli and Coen, 2015
]
,
so an infinite reduction
s\leadsto\leadsto\ldots

must contain an infinite number of
\leadsto_{{\mathtt{u}}{\mathtt{m}},{\mathtt{u}}{\mathtt{e}}}
steps.
By
Lemmas

6.1
and
6.2

this means that there must exist an infinite
\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}
reduction
starting from
\{\!\!\{s\}\!\!\}=t
.
This is impossible because
t
is known to have a normal form
and
\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}
has the diamond property.

Now let
s\leadsto^{k}s^{\prime}
be a reduction to normal form in the
GLAMoUr containing
m
multiplicative,
e
exponential, and
c
administrative steps,
so
k=m+e+c
.
By
Lemmas

6.1
and
6.2

we have
t=\{\!\!\{s\}\!\!\}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}^{\raisebox{-2.84544pt}{{\footnotesize{$m+e$}}}}t^{\prime\prime}\equiv\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}
.
Moreover
t\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}^{\raisebox{-2.84544pt}{{\footnotesize{$n$}}}}t^{\prime}
by hypothesis.
Since
s^{\prime}
is
\leadsto
-normal, we know by
Lemma

6.1

that
\{\!\!\{s^{\prime}\}\!\!\}
is
\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}
-normal, so
t^{\prime\prime}
is also
\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}
-normal.
But
\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}{{\footnotesize$\blacktriangle$}}}
has the diamond property, so
t^{\prime}=t^{\prime\prime}
and
n=m+e
.
To conclude, we are left to show that
k\in O(|t|(n+1))
.
By Lemma 6 in
[
Accattoli and Coen, 2015
]
we know that
c\in O(|t|(e+1))
.
Since
n=m+e
, finally we have that
k=m+e+c\in O(|t|(n+1))
.
∎

Appendix E
Proofs of Section
7
“A Quantitative Interpretation”

In this section we show the results concerning the typing system
\mathcal{U}
.
We start with general lemmas and remarks used through this section, and then
we show soundness and completeness in

Section

E.1
and
Section

E.2
respectively.

See
7.1

Proof.

By induction on
\Phi
. We omit cases
appC
and
es
as they are analogous to case
appP
.

1.

var
.
Then,
\Phi\rhd x:\mathcal{T}\vdash^{(0,{\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}))}x:\mathcal{T}
,
where
\Gamma=x:\mathcal{T}
and
m=0
and
e={\tt ta}(\mathcal{T})

and
t=x
.
Therefore,
\mathsf{dom}(x:\mathcal{T})=\{x\}=\mathsf{rv}(x)=\mathsf{fv}(x)
.

2.

abs
.
Then

\frac{\left(\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{i}\vdash^{(m_{i},e_{i})}u:\mathcal{R}_{i}\right)_{i\in I}}{+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{(+_{i\in I}m_{i},+_{i\in I}e_{i})}\lambda x.\,u:[\mathcal{S}^{?}_{i}\to\mathcal{R}_{i}]_{i\in I}}\textup{{abs}}

where
\Gamma=+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}
and
m=+_{i\in I}m_{i}
and

e=+_{i\in I}e_{i}
and
t=\lambda x.\,u
and

\mathcal{T}=[\mathcal{S}^{?}_{i}\to\mathcal{R}_{i}]_{i\in I}
.

We can apply the
i.h.
on
(\Phi_{i})_{i\in I}
, yielding

\mathsf{rv}(u)\subseteq\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{i})\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(u)

for all
{i\in I}
.
For each
{i\in I}
, we have to separate in cases, depending on whether

\mathcal{S}^{?}_{i}=\bot
.

Since both cases are analogous, we only focus on
the case in which
\mathcal{S}^{?}\neq\bot
.
So we have

\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathsf{rv}(u)\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{i})\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})\cup\{x\}\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(u)
.
By removing
x
from the inequalities, we obtain:

\mathsf{rv}(\lambda x.\,u)=\varnothing\subseteq\mathsf{dom}(+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i})=\bigcup_{i\in I}\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(u)\setminus\{x\}=\mathsf{fv}(\lambda x.\,u)

3.

appP
.
Then

\frac{\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\,\mathbb{s}\hskip 8.5359pt\Phi_{2}\rhd\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathbb{t}}{\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u\,s:\,\mathbb{s}}\textup{{appP}}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and
m=m_{1}+m_{2}
and

e=e_{1}+e_{2}
and
t=u\,s
and

\mathcal{T}=\,\mathbb{s}
.

By the
i.h.
on both
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
, we yield

\mathsf{rv}(u)\subseteq\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(u)
and

\mathsf{rv}(s)\subseteq\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(s)
, respectively.
We conclude that
\mathsf{rv}(u\,s)\subseteq\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})=\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\cup\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(u\,s)
.

∎

Some (simple) properties of the notion of
appropriateness
,
defined in
Section

7
, are the following:

Remark E.1.

1.

If
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(x:\mathcal{T})
and
\mathcal{T}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{T}

then
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(x:\mathcal{T}^{?})
.

2.

If
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)
and for all
x\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime},x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)

then
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}(\Gamma)
.

3.

If
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)
and
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Delta)

then
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma+\Delta)
.

Lemma E.2 (Types of hereditary abstractions).

Let
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathcal{T}

where
t\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}

and
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)
.
Then
\mathcal{T}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathcal{T}
.

1.

var
.
Let
x:\mathcal{T}\vdash^{(0,n)}x:\mathcal{T}
, with
n={\tt ta}(\mathcal{T})
. Moreover,

x\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(x:\mathcal{T})
.
By the premise of rule
h-var
, we know that
x\in\mathcal{A}
, and
thus
\mathcal{T}\neq\,\mathbb{s}

by definition of
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(x:\mathcal{T})
.

2.

abs
.
This case is immediate since the judgement is

+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{(+_{i\in I}m_{i},+_{i\in I}e_{i})}\lambda x.\,t:[\mathcal{T}^{?}_{i}\to\mathcal{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}
,
whose type is not equal to
\,\mathbb{s}
.

3.

appP
.
This case is not possible, since the term is of the form
u\,s
,
and the hypothesis
u\,s\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
does not hold.

4.

appC
.
Analogous to the previous case.

5.

es
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=189.18826pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 24.50818pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u[x\backslash s]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

where

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
,

m=m_{1}+m_{2}
,

e=e_{1}+e_{2}

and
t=u[x\backslash s]
.
Moreover,
u[x\backslash s]\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
, which can be derived either by rule
h-sub
1
or rule
h-sub
2
:

5.1

h-sub
1
.
Then
u\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
x\notin\mathcal{A}
. Given that
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
, then

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?})
.
We can apply
i.h.
on
u
, yielding
\mathcal{T}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
.

5.2

h-sub
2
.
Then
u\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}
,
x\notin\mathcal{A}

and
s\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Given that
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
, then
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{2})
.
We can apply
i.h.
on
s
, yielding
\mathcal{S}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
.
Since
\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}
, we can conclude that
\mathcal{S}^{?}\neq\,\mathbb{s}

so
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?})
holds.
By
i.h.
on
u
,
we have
\mathcal{T}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
.

∎

Lemma E.3 (Splitting / Merging).

Let
\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{T}_{2}
be two types such that
their sum
\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}
is well-defined.
Then the following are equivalent:

1.

\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}v:\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}

2.

There exist
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},m_{1},e_{1},m_{2},e_{2}
such that:

(a)

\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}v:\mathcal{T}_{1}

(b)

\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}v:\mathcal{T}_{2}

(c)

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}

and
m=m_{1}+m_{2}

and
e=e_{1}+e_{2}
.

Proof.

We first show that
(1\Rightarrow 2)
.
The judgement can be derived using rule
var
or rule
abs
:

•

var
.
Then
\Gamma\vdash^{(0,e)}x:\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}
, with

e={\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2})
.
Since the sum of the types is well defined, there are two subcases:

1.

\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2}=\,\mathbb{s}
so that
\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}=\,\mathbb{s}
,
hence
{\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2})=0
. Taking

\Gamma_{1}=x:\,\mathbb{s}
,

\Gamma_{2}=x:\,\mathbb{s}
,

m_{1}=0
,

m_{2}=0
,

e_{1}=0
,

e_{2}=0
,
it’s easy to check that the three conditions hold.

2.

\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{M}_{2}
so that
\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathrel{\uplus}\mathcal{M}_{2}
.
We can write
{\tt ta}(\mathcal{M}_{1}+\mathcal{M}_{2})
as

{\tt ta}(\mathcal{M}_{1})+{\tt ta}(\mathcal{M}_{2})
. Taking

\Gamma_{1}=x:\mathcal{M}_{1}
,

\Gamma_{2}=x:\mathcal{M}_{2}
,

m_{1}=0
,

m_{2}=0
,

e_{1}={\tt ta}(\mathcal{M}_{1})
,

e_{2}={\tt ta}(\mathcal{M}_{2})
,
it’s easy to check the that three conditions hold.

•

abs
.
Then

\Phi:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 31.86629pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty(\Gamma_{i};x:\mathcal{R}^{?}_{i}\vdash^{(m_{i},e_{i})}t:\mathcal{S}_{i})_{i\in I}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=171.64566pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{abs}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{(+_{i\in I}m_{i},+_{i\in I}e_{i})}\lambda x.\,t:[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{i}\to\mathcal{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}$}}}}\end{array}\right)

with

[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{i}\to\mathcal{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}=\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}
.
Since the sum of types is well defined, there are two subcases:

1.

\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{2}=\,\mathbb{s}
so that
\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}=\,\mathbb{s}
.
This case is not possible because the term has the non-idempotent type

[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{i}\to\mathcal{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}
.

2.

\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{M}_{2}
so that
\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{M}_{1}\mathrel{\uplus}\mathcal{M}_{2}
.
Then we can write

I
as
I=J\mathrel{\uplus}K
,
in such a way that

\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{M}_{1}=[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{j}\to\mathcal{S}_{j}]_{j\in J}

and

\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{M}_{2}=[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{k}\to\mathcal{S}_{k}]_{k\in K}
.
In particular,

[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{i}\to\mathcal{S}_{i}]_{i\in I}=[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{j}\to\mathcal{S}_{j}]_{j\in J}+[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{k}\to\mathcal{S}_{k}]_{k\in K}
.
Taking

\Gamma_{1}=+_{{j\in J}}\Gamma_{j}
,

\Gamma_{2}=+_{{k\in K}}\Gamma_{k}
,

m_{1}=+_{j\in J}m_{j}
,

m_{2}=+_{k\in K}m_{k}
,

e_{1}=+_{j\in J}e_{j}
,

e_{2}=+_{k\in K}e_{k}
,
we can check that the three conditions hold:

(a)

+_{j\in J}\Gamma_{j}\vdash^{(+_{j\in J}m_{j},+_{j\in J}e_{j})}\lambda x.\,t:[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{j}\to\mathcal{S}_{j}]_{j\in J}

by
abs
rule, since

(\Gamma_{j};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{j}\vdash^{(m_{j},e_{j})}t:\mathcal{S}_{j})_{j\in J}

holds, as they are
j
premises of
\Phi

(b)

+_{k\in K}\Gamma_{k}\vdash^{(+_{k\in K}m_{k},+_{k\in K}e_{k})}\lambda x.\,t:[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{k}\to\mathcal{S}_{k}]_{k\in K}

by
abs
rule, since

(\Gamma_{k};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{k}\vdash^{(m_{k},e_{k})}t:\mathcal{S}_{k})_{k\in K}

holds, as they are
k
premises of
\Phi

(c)

\Gamma=+_{i\in I}\Gamma_{i}=+_{j\in J}\Gamma_{j}+_{k\in K}\Gamma_{k}=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}

and
m=m_{i\in I}=+_{j\in J}m_{j}+_{k\in K}m_{k}=m_{1}+m_{2}

and
e=e_{i\in I}=+_{j\in J}e_{j}+_{k\in K}e_{k}=e_{1}+e_{2}

Now we can show that
(2\Rightarrow 1)
.
First, note that
v
is either a variable or an abstraction,
so both judgements are derived using rule
var
or rule
abs
.

1.

var
.
Then
x:\mathcal{T}_{1}\vdash^{(0,e_{1})}x:\mathcal{T}_{1}
, with

e_{1}={\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}_{1})
and we also
have
x:\mathcal{T}_{2}\vdash^{(0,e_{2})}x:\mathcal{T}_{2}
, with

e_{2}={\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}_{2})
.
Using the third condition, we can conclude

x:\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}\vdash^{(0,e_{1}+e_{2})}x:\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}

by applying rule
var
, since

e_{1}+e_{2}={\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}_{1})+{\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}_{2})={\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2})
.

2.

abs
. The following conditions hold:

(a)

\Phi_{1}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 34.19762pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty(\Gamma_{j};x:\mathcal{R}^{?}_{j}\vdash^{(m_{j},e_{j})}t:\mathcal{S}_{j})_{j\in J}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=181.09367pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{abs}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle+_{j\in J}\Gamma_{j}\vdash^{(+_{j\in J}m_{j},+_{j\in J}e_{j})}\lambda x.\,t:[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{j}\to\mathcal{S}_{j}]_{j\in J}$}}}}\end{array}\right)

with
\mathcal{T}_{1}=[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{j}\to\mathcal{S}_{j}]_{j\in J}
,

m_{1}=+_{j\in J}m_{j}
,
e_{1}=+_{j\in J}e_{j}

(b)

\Phi_{2}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 37.94365pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty(\Gamma_{k};x:\mathcal{R}^{?}_{k}\vdash^{(m_{k},e_{k})}t:\mathcal{S}_{k})_{k\in K}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=194.75133pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{abs}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle+_{k\in K}\Gamma_{k}\vdash^{(+_{k\in K}m_{k},+_{k\in K}e_{k})}\lambda x.\,t:[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{k}\to\mathcal{S}_{k}]_{k\in K}$}}}}\end{array}\right)

with
\mathcal{T}_{2}=[\mathcal{R}^{?}_{k}\to\mathcal{S}_{k}]_{k\in K}
,

m_{2}=+_{k\in K}m_{k}
,
e_{2}=+_{k\in K}e_{k}
.

(c)

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}=+_{j\in J}\Gamma_{j}+_{k\in K}\Gamma_{k}

and
m=m_{1}+m_{2}=+_{j\in J}m_{j}+_{k\in K}m_{k}

and
e=e_{1}+e_{2}=+_{j\in J}e_{j}+_{k\in K}e_{k}
.

We can conclude
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}\lambda x.\,t:\mathcal{T}_{1}+\mathcal{T}_{2}

by applying rule
abs
, using the derivations
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}

as premises.

∎

Definition E.4 (Typing of substitution contexts).

We extend the type system for typing substitution contexts.
The typing judgements for substitution contexts are written

\Gamma\Vdash^{(m,e)}\mathtt{L}\rhd\Delta
, and the new typing rules are:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 24.61803pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=55.87497pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{emptySubsCtx}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\varnothing\Vdash^{(0,0)}\Diamond\rhd\varnothing$}}}}\end{array}\hskip 14.22636pt\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{1}\Vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}\mathtt{L}\rhd\Delta\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{T}^{?}_{1}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}\lhd\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}t:\mathcal{T}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=220.8873pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{addSubsCtx}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 22.97699pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\Vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}\mathtt{L}[x\backslash t]\rhd\Delta;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}$}}}}\end{array}

Example E.5.

Let
t=x[x\backslash y]
with

y:\mathcal{T}\vdash^{(0,{\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}))}y:\mathcal{T}
, we type

[x\backslash y]
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.25345pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=67.14581pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{emptySubsCtx}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:\bot\Vdash^{(0,0)}\Diamond\rhd\varnothing$}}}}\end{array}\hskip 8.5359pt\bot+\mathcal{T}\lhd\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 33.9296pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=74.49811pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{var}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle y:\mathcal{T}\vdash^{(0,{\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}))}y:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=306.57707pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{addSubsCtx}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 96.15761pt\hbox{$\displaystyle y:\mathcal{T}\Vdash^{(0,{\tt ta}(\mathcal{T}))}[x\backslash y]\rhd x:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

Example E.6.

Let
t=z[x\backslash y]
with

y:\mathbb{t}\vdash^{(0,0)}y:\mathbb{t}
, now we type

[x\backslash y]
as follows:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.25345pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=67.14581pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{emptySubsCtx}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x:\bot\Vdash^{(0,0)}\Diamond\rhd\varnothing$}}}}\end{array}\hskip 8.5359pt\bot+\bot\lhd\mathbb{t}\hskip 8.5359pt\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 22.93518pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\vphantom{{}^{@}}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=52.50928pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{var}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle y:\mathbb{t}\vdash^{(0,0)}y:\mathbb{t}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=281.81049pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{addSubsCtx}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 92.8243pt\hbox{$\displaystyle y:\mathbb{t}\Vdash^{(0,0)}[x\backslash y]\rhd x:\bot$}}}}\end{array}

We prove this extension of the type system still preserves the relevance property:

Lemma E.7 (Relevance for typing substitution contexts).

If
\Gamma\Vdash^{(m,e)}\mathtt{L}\rhd\Delta
,
then
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{L})

and
\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)\subseteq\mathtt{dom}(\mathtt{L})
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of the judgement

\Gamma\Vdash^{(m,e)}\mathtt{L}\rhd\Delta
.

1.

emptySubsCtx
.
The judgement is
\varnothing\Vdash^{(0,0)}\Diamond\rhd\varnothing
, where

\Gamma=\Delta=\varnothing
and
m=e=0
and
\mathtt{L}=\Diamond
.
Then,
\mathsf{dom}(\varnothing)=\varnothing=\mathsf{fv}(\Diamond)

and
\mathsf{dom}(\varnothing)=\varnothing=\mathtt{dom}(\Diamond)
, so we are done.

2.

addSubsCtx
.
Then the judgement is of the form

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{1}\Vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\rhd\Delta^{\prime}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{T}^{?}_{1}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}\lhd\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}t:\mathcal{T}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=226.49841pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{addSubsCtx}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 22.97699pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\Vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash t]\rhd\Delta^{\prime};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and
\Delta=\Delta^{\prime};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}

and
m=m_{1}+m_{2}
and
e=e_{1}+e_{2}
and
\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash t]
.

On the one hand, we have

\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{1})\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{L}^{\prime})

by
i.h.
on
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
.
Removing
x
from the inequation we obtain

\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{1})\setminus\{x\}=\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{L}^{\prime})\setminus\{x\}
, so that:

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\cup\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})&\subseteq&(\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{L}^{\prime})\setminus\{x\})\cup\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})\\
&\subseteq&(\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{L}^{\prime})\setminus\{x\})\cup\mathsf{fv}(t)&\text{(By \lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{lem:relevance})}\\
&=&\mathsf{fv}(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash t])\end{array}

On the other hand,

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\mathsf{dom}(\Delta^{\prime};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2})&\subseteq&\mathsf{dom}(\Delta^{\prime})\cup\{x\}\\
&\subseteq&\mathtt{dom}(\mathtt{L}^{\prime})\cup\{x\}&\text{(By {\it i.h.} on $\mathtt{L}^{\prime}$)}\\
&=&\mathtt{dom}(\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash t])\end{array}

so we are done.

∎

Lemma E.8 (Composition/decomposition).

The following are equivalent:

1.

\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t\mathtt{L}:\mathcal{T}

2.

There exist
\Gamma_{t}
,
\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}
,
\Delta
,
m_{t}
,

e_{t}
,
m_{\mathtt{L}}
, and
e_{\mathtt{L}}
such that:

(a)

\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}\Vdash^{(m_{\mathtt{L}},e_{\mathtt{L}})}\mathtt{L}\rhd\Delta

(b)

\Gamma_{t};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{t},e_{t})}t:\mathcal{T}

(c)

\Gamma=\Gamma_{t}+\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}
and

m=m_{t}+m_{\mathtt{L}}
and

e=e_{t}+e_{\mathtt{L}}
.

Furthermore, in the
(1\Rightarrow 2)
direction,
if
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},t\mathtt{L})}
holds,
then
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)

implies
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}}}(\Delta)
.

Proof.

Both directions of the proof are by induction on
\mathtt{L}
.

(1\Rightarrow 2)

•

\mathtt{L}=\Diamond
.
The judgement is of the form
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathcal{T}
. Taking

\Gamma_{t}:=\Gamma
,
\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}:=\varnothing
,

\Delta:=\varnothing
,
m_{t}:=m
,

e_{t}:=e
,
m_{\mathtt{L}}:=0
, and

e_{\mathtt{L}}:=0
we obtain:

(a)

\varnothing\Vdash^{(0,0)}\Diamond\rhd\varnothing
, by rule
emptySubsCtx

(b)

\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathcal{T}
, by hypothesis

(c)

\Gamma=\Gamma+\varnothing
and
m=m+0
and
e=e+0

Furthermore, it is immediate to conclude that
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\varnothing)
holds, so we are done.

•

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]
.
The judgement is of the form
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]:\mathcal{T}
,
so it can only be derived from rule
es
:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}t\mathtt{L}^{\prime}:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}u:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=193.38968pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 23.11932pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}t\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
,
m=m_{1}+m_{2}
, and

e=e_{1}+e_{2}
.

By
i.h.
on
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
, there exist
\Gamma^{\prime}_{t}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}
,

\Delta^{\prime}
,
m_{t}
,
e_{t}
,
m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}
, and
e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}

such that:

(a’)

\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}\Vdash^{(m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}},e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}})}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\rhd\Delta^{\prime}

(b’)

\Gamma^{\prime}_{t};\Delta^{\prime}\vdash^{(m_{t},e_{t})}t:\mathcal{T}

(c’)

\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}=\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\Gamma^{\prime}_{t}
and

m_{1}=m_{t}+m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}
and

e_{1}=e_{t}+e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}
.

Furthermore,

\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},t\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u])}
implies

\mathsf{inv}{(\tilde{\mathcal{A}},\tilde{\mathcal{S}},t\mathtt{L}^{\prime})}
,
with
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{S}

or
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{A}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}=\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\}
,
so that in either case,

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}(\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?})
implies

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\tilde{\mathcal{A}}^{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}}(\Delta^{\prime})
.

By statement (c’), we can write

\mathcal{S}^{?}
as
\mathcal{S}^{?}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{t}
, so that

\Gamma^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}=\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}
and

\Gamma^{\prime}_{t}=\Gamma_{t};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{t}
and

\mathcal{S}^{?}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{t}\lhd\mathcal{S}
.
We then have
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\Gamma_{t}
.
Taking

\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}:=\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\Gamma_{2}
,

\Gamma_{t}
,

\Delta:=\Delta^{\prime};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{t}
,

m_{t}
,

e_{t}
,

m_{\mathtt{L}}:=m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+m_{2}
, and

e_{\mathtt{L}}=e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+e_{2}
we have:

(a)

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}\Vdash^{(m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}},e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}})}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\rhd\Delta^{\prime}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{t}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}u:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=231.09848pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{addSubsCtx}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 22.03738pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\Gamma_{2}\Vdash^{(m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+m_{2},e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+e_{2})}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]\rhd\Delta^{\prime};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{t}$}}}}\end{array}

(b)

\Gamma_{t};\Delta^{\prime};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{t}\vdash^{(m_{t},e_{t})}t:\mathcal{T}
,
by condition (b’)

(c)

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{t}+\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{t}+\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}

and

m=m_{1}+m_{2}=m_{t}+m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+m_{2}=m_{t}+m_{\mathtt{L}}

and

e=e_{1}+e_{2}=e_{t}+e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+e_{2}=e_{t}+e_{\mathtt{L}}

If
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},t\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u])}
holds,
we want to show that
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)
implies

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]}}(\Delta)
.
We already know that
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}}(\Delta^{\prime})

holds by the
i.h.
on
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
.
We consider two cases:

–

\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]}=\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}\cup\{x\}
.
This means
u\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Moreover,
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{2})
holds, as

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
.
Then
\mathcal{S}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
by
Lemma

E.2
.
By definition

\mathcal{S}^{?}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{t}\lhd\mathcal{S}
implies

\mathcal{S}^{?}_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
and
\mathcal{S}^{?}_{t}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
,
so we can conclude that

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]}}(\Delta)
holds.

–

\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]}=\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}
.
Immediate by the
i.h.
on
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
.

(2\Rightarrow 1)

•

\mathtt{L}=\Diamond
.
Then, there exist

\Gamma_{t}
,
\Gamma_{\Diamond}
,
\Delta
,
m_{t}
,
e_{t}
,

m_{\Diamond}
, and
e_{\Diamond}
such that:

(a)

\Gamma_{\Diamond}\Vdash^{(m_{\Diamond},e_{\Diamond})}\Diamond\rhd\Delta

(b)

\Gamma_{t};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{t},e_{t})}t:\mathcal{T}

(c)

\Gamma=\Gamma_{t}+\Gamma_{\Diamond}
and

m=m_{t}+m_{\Diamond}
and

e=e_{t}+e_{\Diamond}

The judgement from condition (a) can only be derived by rule

emptySubsCtx
, hence
\Gamma_{\Diamond}=\Delta=\varnothing

and
m_{\Diamond}=e_{\Diamond}=0
.
By conditions (b) and (c) it is immediate to conclude

\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathcal{T}
.

•

\mathtt{L}=\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]
.
Then, there exist

\Gamma_{t}
,
\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}
,
\Delta
,
m_{t}
,
e_{t}
,

m_{\mathtt{L}}
, and
e_{\mathtt{L}}
such that:

(a)

\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}\Vdash^{(m_{\mathtt{L}},e_{\mathtt{L}})}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]\rhd\Delta

(b)

\Gamma_{t};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{t},e_{t})}t:\mathcal{T}

(c)

\Gamma=\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}+\Gamma_{t}
and

m=m_{\mathtt{L}}+m_{t}
and

e=e_{\mathtt{L}}+e_{t}

The judgement from condition (a) can only be derived by rule

addSubsCtx
:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{1}\Vdash^{(m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}},e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}})}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\rhd\Delta^{\prime}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}_{1}+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{u}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=228.92339pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{addSubsCtx}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 17.99266pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\Gamma_{u}\Vdash^{(m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+m_{u},e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+m_{u})}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]\rhd\Delta^{\prime};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}=\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\Gamma_{u}
,

\Delta=\Delta^{\prime};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}
,

m_{\mathtt{L}}=m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+m_{2}
, and

e_{\mathtt{L}}=e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+e_{2}
.

Then, there exist

\Sigma_{t}=\Gamma_{t};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}
,

\Sigma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}=\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}_{1}
,

\Delta^{\prime}
,
m_{t}
,
e_{t}
,
m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}
, and
e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}

such that:

(a’)

\Sigma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}\Vdash^{(m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}},e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}})}\mathtt{L}^{\prime}\rhd\Delta^{\prime}

(b’)

\Sigma_{t};\Delta^{\prime}\vdash^{(m_{t},e_{t})}t:\mathcal{T}

(c’)

\Sigma=\Sigma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\Sigma_{t}
and

m^{\prime}=m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+m_{t}
and

e^{\prime}=e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+e_{t}
.

We can apply the
i.h.
on
\mathtt{L}^{\prime}
, yielding

\Sigma\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}t\mathtt{L}^{\prime}:\mathcal{T}
.
Applying rule
es
, we conclude:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+\Gamma_{t};x:(\mathcal{S}^{?}_{1}+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2})\vdash^{(m_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+m_{t},e_{\mathtt{L}^{\prime}}+e_{t})}t\mathtt{L}^{\prime}:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}_{1}+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{u}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=301.01276pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 107.54282pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t\mathtt{L}^{\prime}[x\backslash u]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

∎

Definition E.9.

Let
X
be a finite set of variables.
We say that a context
\Gamma
is
X-
tight
if for all

x\in X
,
\Gamma(x)=\bot
or
\Gamma(x)=\mathbb{t}
.

Lemma E.10.

Let
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},t)}
.
If
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathcal{T}
,
where
\Gamma
is
\mathcal{S}
-tight and
t\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
.
Then
\mathcal{T}
must be tight.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
t\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
.

•

s-var
.
Then
t=x
and
x\in\mathcal{S}
by premise of the rule

s-var
.
The judgement
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}x:\mathcal{T}
can only be derived
by rule
var
, so
\Gamma
is of the form
x:\mathcal{T}
.
The typing context
x:\mathcal{T}
is
\mathcal{S}-
tight by hypothesis,
and we already know that
x\in\mathcal{S}
, hence
\Gamma(x)
must
be tight, so we are done.

•

s-app
.
Then
t=u\,s
and

u\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
by premise of the rule
s-app
.
The judgement
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}u\,s:\mathcal{T}
can be
derived either by rule
appP
or rule
appC
.
The former case is immediate, as the conclusion always type with a
tight type. Hence, we look at the second case, where the typing
derivation is of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:[\mathcal{S}^{?}\to\mathcal{T}]\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=186.80638pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{appC}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 27.77899pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(1+m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u\,s:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
,
m=1+m_{1}+m_{2}
, and

e=e_{1}+e_{2}
.

Moreover,
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u\,s)}
implies

\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)}
, and
\Gamma_{1}
is
\mathcal{S}-
tight since

\Gamma
is
\mathcal{S}-
tight and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
.
Then we have that
[\mathcal{S}^{?}\to\mathcal{T}]
must be tight by
the
i.h.
on
u
, which is impossible and yields to a contradiction.
Hence this case is not possible.

•

s-sub
1
.
Then
t=u[x\backslash s]
, and

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty u\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}\hskip 8.5359ptx\notin\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=59.3368pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{s-sub${}_{1}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 2.34088pt\hbox{$\displaystyle u[x\backslash s]\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}$}}}}\end{array}

The judgement
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}u[x\backslash s]:\mathcal{T}

can only be derived by rule
es
, so the typing derivation is
of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=189.18826pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 24.50818pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u[x\backslash s]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
,
m=m_{1}+m_{2}
, and

e=e_{1}+e_{2}
.

Moreover,
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])}
implies

\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)}
,
and
\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}
is
\mathcal{S}-
tight since
(1)
\Gamma
is
\mathcal{S}-
tight and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
, and
(2)
x\not\in\mathcal{S}
by premise of rule
s-sub
1
.
Furthermore,
u\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
holds
by premise of rule
s-sub
1
.
We can then apply the
i.h.
on
u
, concluding that
\mathcal{T}
is
tight, so we are done.

•

s-sub
2
.
Then
t=u[x\backslash s]
, and

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty u\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\}}\hskip 8.5359ptx\notin\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=116.01164pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{s-sub${}_{2}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 30.6783pt\hbox{$\displaystyle u[x\backslash s]\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}$}}}}\end{array}

The judgement
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}u[x\backslash s]:\mathcal{T}

can only be derived by rule
es
, so the typing derivation is
of the form:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=189.18826pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 24.50818pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u[x\backslash s]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
,
m=m_{1}+m_{2}
, and

e=e_{1}+e_{2}
.

By Barendregt’s convention, we may assume
x\notin\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{S}
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])}
implies

\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)}
.
Furthermore,
\Gamma_{2}
is
\mathcal{S}-
tight, since
\Gamma
is

\mathcal{S}-
tight and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
.
By premise of the rule
s-sub
2
,

s\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
holds.
We can then apply the
i.h.
on
s
, yielding that
\mathcal{S}
is tight.

On the other hand,
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])}

implies
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\},u)}
, and

\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}
is
(\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\})-
tight since
(1)
\mathcal{S}
is tight and
\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}
by premise of rule
es
,
so
\mathcal{S}^{?}
must be either
\bot
or
\mathbb{t}
, and
(2)
\Gamma
is
(\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\})-
tight and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
.
By premise of rule
s-sub
2
,

u\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\}}
holds.
Applying the
i.h.
on
u
, we conclude that
\mathcal{T}
is tight,
so we are done.

∎

Lemma E.11.

Let
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},t)}
, and suppose that the following hold:

1.

\Gamma;\Delta\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathbb{t}

2.

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)
, with
\Gamma
tight

3.

\Delta\neq\varnothing

4.

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)\subseteq\mathcal{A}

5.

For all
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)
,
\Delta(x)
is a non-empty multiset.

Then,
t\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of the judgement

\Gamma;\Delta\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathbb{t}
.

•

var
.
Then,
t=x
and the typing judgement is of the form

x:\mathbb{t}\vdash^{(0,0)}x:\mathbb{t}
, where

\Gamma;\Delta=x:\mathbb{t}
,
m=0
, and
e=0
.
Moreover,
\Delta\neq\varnothing
by hypothesis, so

\Delta=x:\mathbb{t}
.
But we reach a contradiction since
\Delta(x)
is a non-empty multiset
by condition 5. of the hypothesis.
Thus, this case does not apply.

•

abs
.
Then,
t=\lambda x.\,u
and the typing judgement is of the form

\Gamma;\Delta\vdash^{(m,e)}\lambda x.\,u:\mathbb{t}
, that can only
be derived by rule
abs
.
Hence, it must be the case that
\mathbb{t}=[]
and the conclusion
of the typing derivation holds with no premises, so we have in particular

\Gamma;\Delta=\varnothing
.
But we reach a contradiction since
\Delta\neq\varnothing
by
hypothesis.
Thus, this case does not apply.

•

appP
.
Then,
t=u\,s
, and the following hold:

1.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 7.36398pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};\Delta_{1}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\,\mathbb{s}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2};\Delta_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathbb{t}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=177.92888pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{appP}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2});(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u\,s:\,\mathbb{s}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
,
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
,

m=m_{1}+m_{2}
,
e=e_{1}+e_{2}
, and
\mathbb{t}=\,\mathbb{s}

2.

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})
, with
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
tight

3.

\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing

4.

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{A}

5.

For all
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1}+\Gamma_{2})
,

(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})(x)
is a non-empty multiset.

Moreover,
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u\,s)}
implies
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)}

and
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)}
.
By condition 3., we have to analyze two possible cases:

–

If
\Delta_{1}\neq\varnothing
, we state that the following hold:

1’.

\Gamma_{1};\Delta_{1}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\,\mathbb{s}
, by
premise of the typing derivation in condition 1

2’.

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{1})
, with
\Gamma_{1}
tight, since

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and condition 2

3’.

\Delta_{1}\neq\varnothing
, by condition 3

4’.

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1})\subseteq\mathcal{A}
, since

\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and condition 4

5’.

For all
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1})
,
\Delta_{1}(x)
is a
non-empty multiset, since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}

and condition 5.

We can apply the
i.h.
on
u
, yielding

u\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
, which in particular holds
when
\mu=@
.
By definition, there exist
\rho
and
u^{\prime}
such that

u\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},@}u^{\prime}
.
We can apply rule
appL
∙
, yielding

u\,s\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}u^{\prime}\,s
,
and by definition we can conclude that

u\,s\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

–

If
\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing
, we state that the following hold:

1’.

\Gamma_{2};\Delta_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathbb{t}
, by
premise of the typing derivation in condition 1

2’.

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{2})
, with
\Gamma_{2}
tight, since

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and condition 2

3’.

\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing
, by condition 3

4’.

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{A}
, since

\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and condition 4

5’.

For all
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{2})
,
\Delta_{2}(x)
is a
non-empty multiset, since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}

and condition 5.

We can apply the
i.h.
on
s
, yielding

s\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
, which in particular holds
when
\mu={\text{$\not$\!@}}
.
By definition, there exist
\rho
and
s^{\prime}
such that

s\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},{\text{$\not$\!@}}}s^{\prime}
.
We reason by contradiction, assuming

u\,s\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.
This assumption can only be derived by rule
NF-app
∙
, so we have

u\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},@}
in particular.
By
Lemma

B.4
, then
u\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
,
so we can apply rule
appR
∙
, yielding

u\,s\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}u\,s^{\prime}
,
which contradicts the assumption.
We conclude that
u\,s\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

•

appC
.
Then
t=u\,s
, and the following hold:

1.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};\Delta_{1}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:[\mathcal{T}^{?}\to\mathbb{t}]\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{T}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2};\Delta_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathcal{T}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=224.66748pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{appC}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 19.16788pt\hbox{$\displaystyle(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2});(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})\vdash^{(1+m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u\,s:\mathbb{t}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
,
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
,

m=1+m_{1}+m_{2}
, and
e=e_{1}+e_{2}

2.

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})
, with
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
tight

3.

\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing

4.

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{A}

5.

For all
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})
,

(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})(x)
is a non-empty multiset.

Moreover,
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u\,s)}
implies

\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)}
and
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)}
.
We reason by contradiction, assuming
u\,s\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.
This assumption can only be derived by rule
NF-app
∙
, so in particular
we have
u\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},@}
.
By
Lemma

B.4
, we have
u\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
.
Moreover,
\Gamma_{1};\Delta_{1}
is
\mathcal{S}-
tight since
(1)
\Gamma_{1}
is tight by
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and condition (b), and
(2)
\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1})\subseteq\mathcal{A}
by condition (d).
We can then apply
Lemma

E.10
, yielding that
[\mathcal{T}^{?}\to\mathbb{t}]
must be tight,
which gives a contradiction since it is a singleton.
We can then conclude that
u\,s\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

•

es
.
Then
t=u[x\backslash s]
, and the following hold:

1.

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};\Delta_{1};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\mathbb{t}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{T}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2};\Delta_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathcal{T}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=227.04936pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 15.89705pt\hbox{$\displaystyle(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2});(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u[x\backslash s]:\mathbb{t}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
,
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
,

m=m_{1}+m_{2}
, and
e=e_{1}+e_{2}

2.

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})
, with
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
tight

3.

\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing

4.

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{A}

5.

For all
y\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})
,

(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})(y)
is a non-empty multiset.

Moreover,
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])}
implies in
particular
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)}
.
We reason by contradiction, assuming
u[x\backslash s]\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.
This assumption can be derived either by rule
NF-esA
∙
or by rule
NF-esS
∙
.
We may assume
x\notin(\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{S})
by Barendregt’s convention.

1.

NF-esA
∙
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty u\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},{\text{$\not$\!@}}}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=171.916pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{NF-esA${}^{\bullet}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 49.17842pt\hbox{$\displaystyle u[x\backslash s]\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}$}}}}\end{array}

By condition (c),
\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing
.
Also,
\mathcal{T}
can be tight or not.
We have to analyze three possible cases:

1.1

\mathcal{T}
not tight.
By condition (a), then
\mathcal{T}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{T}
.
Since
\mathcal{T}
is not tight, then
\mathcal{T}^{?}=\mathcal{T}
.
This means that
\mathcal{T}^{?}\neq\bot
.
We have that the following holds:

(a’)

\Gamma_{1};(\Delta_{1};x:\mathcal{T})\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\mathbb{t}
, by condition (a)

(b’)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma_{1})
, with
\Gamma_{1}
tight,
since
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and condition (b)

(c’)

(\Delta_{1};x:\mathcal{T})\neq\varnothing
since at least
(\Delta_{1};x:\mathcal{T})(x)\neq\bot

(d’)

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1};x:\mathcal{T})\subseteq\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}

since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and condition (d)

(e’)

For all
y\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1};x:\mathcal{T})
, we have
that
(\Delta_{1};x:\mathcal{T})(y)
is a non-empty multiset
since
\mathcal{T}
is not tight,
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and by condition (e).

We can apply
i.h.
on
u
, yielding

u\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}
,
which leads to a contradiction with our assumption.
Therefore
u[x\backslash s]\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

1.2

\mathcal{T}=\mathbb{t}
and
\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing
.
We have that the following holds:

(a’)

\Gamma_{2};\Delta_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathbb{t}
, by condition (a)

(b’)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{2})
, with
\Gamma_{2}
tight since
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and condition (b)

(c’)

\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing
by hypothesis

(d’)

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{A}
since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and condition (e)

(e’)

For all
y\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{2})
, we have that
\Delta_{2}(y)
is a non-empty multiset
since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and condition (e).

We can apply
i.h.
on
s
yielding

s\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
, in particular
\mu={\text{$\not$\!@}}
,
which leads to a contradiction with our assumption.
Therefore
u[x\backslash s]\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

1.3

\mathcal{T}=\mathbb{t},\Delta_{1}\neq\varnothing
and
\Delta_{2}=\varnothing
.
We have that
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])}
implies
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)}
,
and that the following holds:

(a’)

\Gamma_{1};(\Delta_{1};x:\mathcal{T}^{?})\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\mathbb{t}
, by condition (a)

(b’)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{T}^{?})
, as

(1)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma_{1})
.
Indeed
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and condition (b) and
Remark

E.1
;

(2)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(x:\mathcal{T}^{?})
.
It holds that
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{2};\Delta_{2})
, since

\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and conditions (b) and (e).
Then
\mathcal{T}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
by applying
Lemma

E.2

on the derivation of
s
.
Therefore we are in the case where
\mathcal{T}^{?}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
.

Moreover,
\Gamma_{1}
is tight since
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and condition (b).

(c’)

\Delta_{1}\neq\varnothing
by hypothesis

(d’)

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1})\subseteq\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}

since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and condition (d)

(e’)

For all
y\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1})
, we have that
\Delta_{1}(y)
is a non-empty multiset
since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and condition (e).

We can apply
i.h.
on
u
yielding

u\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}
,
which leads to a contradiction with our assumption.
Therefore
u[x\backslash s]\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

2.

NF-esS
∙
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty u\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\},\mu}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},{\text{$\not$\!@}}}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=165.8465pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{NF-esS${}^{\bullet}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 46.14368pt\hbox{$\displaystyle u[x\backslash s]\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}$}}}}\end{array}

By condition (c),
\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing

so we have to analyze two possible cases:

2.1

\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing
.
The following holds:

(a’)

\Gamma_{2};\Delta_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathbb{t}
, by condition (a)

(b’)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{2})
, with
\Gamma_{2}
tight since
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and by condition (b)

(c’)

\Delta_{2}\neq\varnothing
by hypothesis

(d’)

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{2})\subseteq\mathcal{A}
since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and by condition (d)

(e’)

For all
y\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{2})
, we have that
\Delta_{2}(y)
is a non-empty multiset
since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and by condition (e).

We can apply
i.h.
on
s
yielding

s\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
, in particular
\mu={\text{$\not$\!@}}
,
which leads to a contradiction with our assumption.
We then conclude
u[x\backslash s]\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

2.2

\Delta_{2}=\varnothing
(and thus
\Delta_{1}\neq\varnothing
).
We have that
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])}
implies
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\},u)}
,
and that the following holds:

(a’)

\Gamma_{1};\Delta_{1};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\mathbb{t}
, by condition (a)

(b’)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{T}^{?})
, with
\Gamma_{1}
tight, since

x\notin\mathcal{A}
,
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
and by condition (b)

(c’)

\Delta_{1}\neq\varnothing
by hypothesis

(d’)

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1})\subseteq\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}
since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and by condition (d)

(e’)

For all
y\in\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1})
, we have that
\Delta_{1}(y)
is a non-empty multiset
since
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
and by condition (e).

We can apply
i.h.
on
u
yielding

u\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\},\mu}
,
which leads to a contradiction with our assumption.
Therefore
u[x\backslash s]\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

∎
