Lemma E.12.

Let
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},t)}
and suppose the following hypothesis hold:
(1)
t\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
;
(2)
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathbb{t}
is tight;
(3)
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)
;
(4) If
\mu=@
then
\mathbb{t}=\,\mathbb{s}
.
Then
(m,e)=(0,0)
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation
of
t\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.
∎

E.1.
Soundness of
\mathcal{U}

This subsection aims to give the main results regarding the soundness of the
type system
\mathcal{U}
with respect to the
\textup{{ucbv}}^{\bullet}
strategy.
In order to prove this result, stated in
Theorem

7.4
, we need to
show that the subject reduction property (
Proposition

7.3
) holds.
For doing that, we start by presenting the substitution lemma for
\mathcal{U}

in
Lemma

E.13
.

Lemma E.13 (Substitution).

Suppose that the following conditions hold:

(a)

t\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}t^{\prime}

(b)

\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathcal{S}

(c)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?})

(d)

If
\mu=@

then either
\mathcal{S}=\,\mathbb{s}

or
\mathcal{S}
is a singleton, i.e., of the form
[\alpha]
.

Then there exist
\alpha
and
\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}

such that
\mathcal{T}^{?}=[\alpha]+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}

and such that,
whenever
\Delta\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}v:[\alpha]
,
we have that
e>0

and

\Gamma+\Delta;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}\vdash^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-1)}t^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

t\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}t^{\prime}
.

1.

sub
∙
. The following conditions hold:

(a)

x\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},@}v

(b)

\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}\vdash^{(m,e)}x:\mathcal{S}

(c)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?})

(d)

Since
\mu=@
,
either
\mathcal{S}=\,\mathbb{s}

or
\mathcal{S}
is a singleton

The judgement of condition (b)
can only be derived by the rule
var
, hence

\mathcal{T}^{?}=\mathcal{S}
,
\Gamma=\varnothing
,
m=0
and
e={\tt ta}(\mathcal{S})
.
Furthermore,

\mathcal{S}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
,
since
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(x:\mathcal{S})
holds. This implies by hypothesis
that
\mathcal{S}
is a singleton
[\alpha]
for some type
\alpha
.
Thus
e=1
.
Taking such
\alpha
and
\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}=\bot
,
whenever
\Delta\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}v:[\alpha]
,
we can conclude that
\varnothing+\Delta\vdash^{(0+m^{\prime},1+e^{\prime}-1)}v:[\alpha]
.

2.

appL
∙
. The following conditions hold:

(a)

u\,s\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}u^{\prime}\,s
,
with
u\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},@}u^{\prime}
.

(b)

\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}\vdash^{(m,e)}u\,s:\mathcal{S}

(c)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?})

(d)

If
\mu=@

then either
\mathcal{S}=\,\mathbb{s}

or
\mathcal{S}
is a singleton

The judgement
\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}\vdash^{(m,e)}u\,s:\mathcal{S}

can be derived either by rule
appP
or rule
appC
:

2.1

appP
.
Then

\Phi:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{u};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u:\,\mathbb{s}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{s};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s}\vdash^{(m_{s},e_{s})}s:\mathbb{t}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=186.13133pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{appP}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 1.88254pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{u}+\Gamma_{s};x:(\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s})\vdash^{(m_{u}+m_{s},e_{u}+e_{s})}u\,s:\,\mathbb{s}$}}}}\end{array}\right)

with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{u}+\Gamma_{s}
,

\mathcal{T}^{?}=\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s}
,

m=m_{u}+m_{s}
and
e=e_{u}+e_{s}
.
The following holds:

(a’)

u\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu_{u}}u^{\prime}
,
with
\mu_{u}=@

by condition (a)

(b’)

\Gamma_{u};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u:\,\mathbb{s}
,
by condition (b)

(c’)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma_{u};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u})
,
since
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?})
holds,
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{u}+\Gamma_{s}
,

\mathcal{T}^{?}=\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s}
,
and by condition (c)

(d’)

Here
\mu_{u}=@

and the type of
u
is
\,\mathbb{s}
.

We can apply
i.h.
on
u
, yielding
\alpha
and
\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}
such that

\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}=[\alpha]+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}
and such that, whenever

\Delta\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}v:[\alpha]
, we have that
e_{u}>0
and

\Gamma_{u}+\Delta;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m^{\prime},e_{u}+e^{\prime}-1)}u^{\prime}:\,\mathbb{s}
.
Then, taking this judgement and the second premise of
\Phi
,
we can apply rule
appP
, yielding

\Gamma+\Delta;x:(\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s})\vdash^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-1)}u^{\prime}\,s:\,\mathbb{s}
,
with
e>0
because
e=e_{u}+e_{s}
, and
e_{u}>0
by
i.h.
We take
\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}=\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s}
,
and we can conclude that
\mathcal{T}^{?}=[\alpha]+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}
holds.

2.2

appC
.
Then

\Phi:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{u};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u:[\mathcal{R}^{?}\to\mathcal{S}]\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{R}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{R}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{s};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s}\vdash^{(m_{s},e_{s})}s:\mathcal{R}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=249.82011pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{appC}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 28.69215pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{u}+\Gamma_{s};x:(\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s})\vdash^{(1+m_{u}+m_{s},e_{u}+e_{s})}u\,s:\mathcal{S}$}}}}\end{array}\right)

with
\Gamma=\Gamma_{u}+\Gamma_{s}
,

\mathcal{T}^{?}=\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s}
,

m=1+m_{u}+m_{s}
and
e=e_{u}+e_{s}
.
The following holds:

(a’)

u\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu_{u}}u^{\prime}

with
\mu_{u}=@

by condition (a)

(b’)

\Gamma_{u};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}\vdash^{(m_{u},e_{u})}u:[\mathcal{R}^{?}\to\mathcal{S}]
,
by condition (b)

(c’)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma_{u};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u})
,
since
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?})
holds,
and
\Gamma=\Gamma_{u}+\Gamma_{s}
, and by condition (c)

\mathcal{T}^{?}=\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s}

(d’)

We have
\mu_{u}=@
and the type of

is the singleton

[\mathcal{R}^{?}\to\mathcal{S}]
.

We cann apply
i.h.
on
u
, yielding
\alpha
and
\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}
such that

\mathcal{T}^{?}_{u}=[\alpha]+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}
and such that, whenever

\Delta\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}v:[\alpha]
, we have that
e_{u}>0
and

\Gamma_{u}+\Delta;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}\vdash^{(m_{u}+m^{\prime},e_{u}+e^{\prime}-1)}u^{\prime}:[\mathcal{R}^{?}\to\mathcal{S}]
.
Then, taking this judgement and the second premise of
\Phi
,
we can apply rule
appC
, yielding

\Gamma+\Delta;x:(\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s})\vdash^{(m+m^{\prime},e+e^{\prime}-1)}u^{\prime}\,s:\mathcal{S}
,
with

because

, and

by

We take
\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}=\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s2}+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{s}

and we can conclude
\mathcal{T}^{?}=[\alpha]+\mathcal{T}^{?}_{2}
holds.

3.

The remaining cases are similar.

∎

See
7.3

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of

t\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho,\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}t^{\prime}
.

1.

db
∙
.
The following conditions hold:

(a)

(\lambda x.\,u)\mathtt{L}\,s\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{db},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}u[x\backslash s]\mathtt{L}

(b)

\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}(\lambda x.\,u)\mathtt{L}\,s:\mathcal{T}

(c)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)

(d)

If
\mu=@

then either
\mathcal{T}=\,\mathbb{s}

or
\mathcal{T}
is a singleton,
i.e.
, of the form
[\alpha]
.

The judgement of condition (b) can be derived either by rule

or rule

.

1.1

appP
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}(\lambda x.\,u)\mathtt{L}:\,\mathbb{s}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathbb{t}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=154.9162pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{appP}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 3.1916pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}(\lambda x.\,u)\mathtt{L}\,s:\,\mathbb{s}$}}}}\end{array}

,

,

, there exist

This leads to a contradiction, since an abstraction cannot have type

.
Then this case is not possible.

1.2

appC
.
Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}(\lambda x.\,u)\mathtt{L}:[\mathcal{S}^{?}\to\mathcal{T}]\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=213.04947pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{appC}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 26.39014pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(1+m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}(\lambda x.\,u)\mathtt{L}\,s:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

where

,

,

,
and

, there exist

\Gamma_{11},\Gamma_{12},\Delta,m_{11},e_{11},m_{12},e_{12}

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 8.19443pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{11};\Delta;x:\mathcal{S}^{?}\vdash^{(m_{11},e_{11})}u:\mathcal{T}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=128.55942pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{abs}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{11};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{11},e_{11})}\lambda x.\,u:[\mathcal{S}^{?}\to\mathcal{T}]$}}}}\end{array}

Then we build the following derivation:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{11};\Delta;x:\mathcal{S}^{?}\vdash^{(m_{11},e_{11})}u:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}s:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=212.75783pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 23.11931pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{11}+\Gamma_{2};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{11}+m_{2},e_{11}+e_{2})}u[x\backslash s]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

By applying

E.8
again, we can conclude that

\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u[x\backslash s]\mathtt{L}:\mathcal{T}
.
In this case
\rho=\mathsf{db}
, and
with

, and

(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})=(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})=(m-1,e)
.

2.

lsv
∙
.
The following conditions hold:

(a)

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty u\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}u^{\prime}\hskip 8.5359ptx\notin\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359ptv\mathtt{L}\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=183.09894pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{lsv${}^{\bullet}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 32.8284pt\hbox{$\displaystyle u[x\backslash v\mathtt{L}]\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{lsv},\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}u^{\prime}[x\backslash v]\mathtt{L}$}}}}\end{array}

(b)

\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}u[x\backslash v\mathtt{L}]:\mathcal{T}

(c)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Gamma)

(d)

\mu=@

then either
\mathcal{T}=\,\mathbb{s}

or
\mathcal{T}

, of the form

.

The judgement of condition (b) can only be derived by the rule
es
,
so

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}\vdash^{(m_{1},e_{1})}u:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{2},e_{2})}v\mathtt{L}:\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=192.17902pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 23.11932pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{2},e_{1}+e_{2})}u[x\backslash v\mathtt{L}]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
,

,

,
and

.
By

E.8
, there exist

\Gamma_{21},\Gamma_{22},\Delta,m_{21},e_{21},m_{22},e_{22}
such that

\Gamma_{21};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{21},e_{21})}v:\mathcal{S}

and
\Gamma_{22}\Vdash^{(m_{22},e_{22})}\mathtt{L}\rhd\Delta
, with

\Gamma_{2}=\Gamma_{21}+\Gamma_{22}
,

m_{2}=m_{21}+m_{22}
and

e_{2}=e_{21}+e_{22}
.

Moreover, we can assume

x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)

by
\alpha
-conversion
so that
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma)
also holds.
Therefore
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma_{1};x:\mathcal{S}^{?})

holds since (1):
\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma)
and
\Gamma_{1}
is part
of
\Gamma
, and (2):
v\mathtt{L}\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
by condition (a),
so
\mathcal{S}\neq\,\mathbb{s}

by
Lemma

E.2

and then
\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}

in condition (b)
implies
\mathcal{S}^{?}\neq\,\mathbb{s}
.
Then, we have all the conditions to apply
Lemma

E.13
,
yielding
\alpha
and
\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}
such that

\mathcal{S}^{?}=[\alpha]+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}
,
hence
\mathcal{S}^{?}=\mathcal{S}
, thus
\mathcal{S}=[\alpha]+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}
.
We have two cases, depending on whether
\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}
is
\bot
or not:

2.1

\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}=\bot
. Then,
\mathcal{S}=[\alpha]+\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}=[\alpha]=[\alpha]+[]
.
Recall that
\Gamma_{21};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{21},e_{21})}v:[\alpha]

holds and note that moreover
\varnothing\vdash^{(0,0)}v:[]
.
By
Lemma

E.13

we have that
e_{1}>0
and

\Gamma_{1}+(\Gamma_{21};\Delta);x:\bot\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{21},e_{1}+e_{21}-1)}u^{\prime}:\mathcal{T}
.
Then we build the following derivation:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}+(\Gamma_{21};\Delta);x:\bot\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{21},e_{1}+e_{21}-1)}u^{\prime}:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\bot\lhd[]\hskip 8.5359pt\varnothing\vdash^{(0,0)}v:[]$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=268.91515pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 46.19565pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{21};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{21},e_{1}+e_{21}-1)}u^{\prime}[x\backslash v]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

2.2

\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}\neq\bot
.
Then
\mathcal{S}^{?}_{2}=\mathcal{S}_{2}

so we can write
\mathcal{S}=[\alpha]+\mathcal{S}_{2}
.
Hence we have
\Gamma_{21};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{21},e_{21})}v:[\alpha]+\mathcal{S}_{2}
.
We can apply
Lemma

E.3
, so that there exists

\Gamma^{\prime}_{211}=\Gamma_{211};\Delta_{1},\Gamma^{\prime}_{212}=\Gamma_{212};\Delta_{2}
,

m_{211},m_{212},e_{211},e_{212}
such that

\Gamma^{\prime}_{211}\vdash^{(m_{211},e_{211})}v:[\alpha]
and

\Gamma^{\prime}_{212}\vdash^{(m_{212},e_{212})}v:\mathcal{S}_{2}
,
with

\Gamma_{21}=\Gamma_{211}+\Gamma_{212}

and
\Delta=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}
,
and
m_{21}=m_{211}+m_{212}
and
e_{21}=e_{211}+e_{212}
.
So by
Lemma

E.13
,
whenever

\Gamma_{211};\Delta_{1}\vdash^{(m_{211},e_{211})}v:[\alpha]
, we have that
e_{1}>0
and

\Gamma_{1}+(\Gamma_{211};\Delta_{1});x:\mathcal{S}_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{211},e_{1}+e_{211}-1)}u^{\prime}:\mathcal{T}
.
Then we build the following derivation:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma_{1}+(\Gamma_{211};\Delta_{1});x:\mathcal{S}_{2}\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{211},e_{1}+e_{211}-1)}u^{\prime}:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}_{2}\lhd\mathcal{S}_{2}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{212};\Delta_{2}\vdash^{(m_{212},e_{212})}v:\mathcal{S}_{2}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=343.00063pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 83.23839pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{21};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{21},e_{1}+e_{21}-1)}u^{\prime}[x\backslash v]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

We can now apply
Lemma

E.8
using the judgements

\Gamma_{22}\Vdash^{(m_{22},e_{22})}\mathtt{L}\rhd\Delta
and

\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{21};\Delta\vdash^{(m_{1}+m_{21},e_{1}+e_{21}-1)}u^{\prime}[x\backslash v]:\mathcal{T}
,
yielding

\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e-1)}u^{\prime}[x\backslash v]\mathtt{L}:\mathcal{T}
.
In this case
\rho=\mathsf{lsv}
and it holds that

e>0
since
e_{1}>0
and
e=e_{1}+e_{2}
.
Moreover,
(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})=(m,e-1)
, so we are done.

3.

The congruence cases are uninteresting and are omitted here.

∎

See
7.4

Proof.

By induction on
m+e
, separating in cases depending of whether

t\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}
:

1.

If
t\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}
,
we also have that the judgement
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathcal{T}
is tight,

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\varnothing}(\Gamma)
, and
\mu={\text{$\not$\!@}}
.
We can apply
Lemma

E.12
, yielding
m=0,e=0
.
Then we conclude with
u:=t
.

2.

If
t\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}
,
then
by
Corollary

B.11

there exist a term
t^{\prime}
and a rule name
\rho

such that

t\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho,\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}t^{\prime}
.
By Subject reduction (
Proposition

7.3
), we have that

\Gamma\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}t^{\prime}:\mathcal{T}
,
with
m>0
and
(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})=(m-1,e)
if
\rho=\mathsf{db}
,
and if
\rho=\mathsf{lsv}
then
e>0
and
(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})=(m,e-1)
.
Since the judgement is tight, we can apply
i.h.
,
so there exists a term
u\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t^{\prime}),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}
such that

t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho_{1},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t^{\prime}),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}\ldots\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho_{n},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t^{\prime}),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}u
,
where
n=m+e-1
and

m^{\prime}=\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq n,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{db}\}\hskip 14.22636pte^{\prime}=\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq n,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{lsv}\}

Then we have a reduction sequence. If we rename
\rho
by
\rho_{n+1}
, we can conclude that

t\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho_{n+1},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho_{1},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}\ldots\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho_{n},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}u

where

1+n=m+e
and if

•

\rho_{n+1}=\mathsf{db}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}m&=&(m-1)+1\\
&=&m^{\prime}+1\\
&=&\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq 1,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{db}\}+1\\
&=&\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq 1+n,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{db}\}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{rcl}e&=&e^{\prime}\\
&=&\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq n,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{lsv}\}\\
&=&\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq 1+n,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{lsv}\}\end{array}

•

\rho_{n+1}=\mathsf{lsv}
:

\begin{array}[]{rcl}m&=&m^{\prime}\\
&=&\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq n,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{db}\}\\
&=&\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq 1+n,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{db}\}\end{array}\begin{array}[]{rcl}e&=&(e-1)+1\\
&=&e^{\prime}+1\\
&=&\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq n,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{lsv}\}+1\\
&=&\#\{i\ |\ 1\leq i\leq 1+n,\rho_{i}=\mathsf{lsv}\}\end{array}

∎

E.2.
Completeness of
\mathcal{U}

In this subsection we give the main results regarding the completeness of
the type system
\mathcal{U}
with respect to the
\textup{{ucbv}}^{\bullet}
strategy.
In order to prove this result, stated in
Theorem

7.7
, we need to
show that the subject expansion property (
Proposition

7.6
) holds.
For doing that, we start by
presenting the anti-substitution lemma for
\mathcal{U}
in
Lemma

E.14
.
Moreover, given that we are working with a tight typing system, we also need
to show that normal forms of
\textup{{ucbv}}^{\bullet}
are tight typable, as stated in
Proposition

7.5
.

See
7.5

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
t\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}
.

1.

NF-var
∙
. Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty x\in\mathcal{A}\Rightarrow\mu={\text{$\not$\!@}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=65.62958pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{NF-var${}^{\bullet}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 8.74123pt\hbox{$\displaystyle x\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}$}}}}\end{array}

There are two possible cases depending on
whether
x\in\mathcal{A}
or
x\in\mathcal{S}
.

1.1

x\in\mathcal{A}
.
By definition
\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},x)(x)=[]
,
since
x\in\mathcal{A}\cap\mathsf{rv}(x)
and

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},x)(y)=\bot
for any other variable
y
.
Having
{\tt ta}([])=0
,
we apply rule
var
, yielding

x:[]\vdash^{(0,0)}x:[]
,
with
x\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
by rule
h-var
.

1.2

x\in\mathcal{S}
.
By definition
\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},x)(x)=\,\mathbb{s}
,
since
x\in\mathcal{S}\cap\mathsf{rv}(x)
and

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},x)(y)=\bot
for any other variable
y
.
Having
{\tt ta}(\,\mathbb{s})=0
,
we apply rule
var
, yielding

x:\,\mathbb{s}\vdash^{(0,0)}x:\,\mathbb{s}
,
with
x\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
by rule
s-var
.

2.

NF-lam
∙
. Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 34.75471pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=69.50943pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{NF-lam${}^{\bullet}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\lambda x.\,u\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},{\text{$\not$\!@}}}$}}}}\end{array}

For any variable
y
, we have

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\lambda x.\,u)(y)=\bot

since
\mathsf{rv}(\lambda x.\,u)=\varnothing
.
We apply rule
abs
, yielding

\vdash^{(0,0)}\lambda x.\,u:[]
,
with
\lambda x.\,u\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
by rule
h-lam
.

3.

NF-app
∙
. Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty u\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},@}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},{\text{$\not$\!@}}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=110.44826pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{NF-app${}^{\bullet}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 27.96884pt\hbox{$\displaystyle u\,s\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}$}}}}\end{array}

Since
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u\,s)}
implies

\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)}
and
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)}
,
then by
i.h.
on
u
and
s

there exist tight types
\mathbb{t}_{1}
and
\mathbb{t}_{2}
such that

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)\vdash^{(0,0)}u:\mathbb{t}_{1}
and

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)\vdash^{(0,0)}s:\mathbb{t}_{2}
.
Moreover,
u\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
by
Lemma

B.4
,
so
\mathbb{t}_{1}=\,\mathbb{s}
.
We apply rule
appP
, yielding

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)+\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)\vdash^{(0,0)}u\,s:\,\mathbb{s}
, with
u\,s\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
by rule
s-app
.
Notice that

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)+\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)=\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u\,s)
,
so we are done.

4.

NF-esA
∙
. Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty u\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},{\text{$\not$\!@}}}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=171.916pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{NF-esA${}^{\bullet}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 49.17842pt\hbox{$\displaystyle u[x\backslash s]\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}$}}}}\end{array}

Since
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])}
implies

\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)}
and
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)}
,
then by
i.h.
on
u
and
s
there exist
tight types
\mathbb{t}_{1}
and
\mathbb{t}_{2}
such that

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)\vdash^{(0,0)}u:\mathbb{t}_{1}
and

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)\vdash^{(0,0)}s:\mathbb{t}_{2}
. Moreover,

\mathbb{t}_{2}=[]
since
s\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}}
.
Notice that
\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)=\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u);x:\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)(x)
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)(x)
is
[]
if
x\in\mathsf{rv}(u)
, and
\bot
otherwise.
We build the following derivation:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 86.00429pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\text{(By {\it i.h.})}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=209.7919pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u);x:\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)(x)\vdash^{(0,0)}u:\mathbb{t}_{1}$}}}}\end{array}\hskip 8.5359pt\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 30.98332pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\text{(By {\it i.h.})}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=99.74997pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)\vdash^{(0,0)}s:[]$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=348.07779pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 77.75528pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)+\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)\vdash^{(0,0)}u[x\backslash s]:\mathbb{t}_{1}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)(x)\lhd[]
necessarily holds since

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)(x)
is
\bot
or
[]
.
Notice that

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)+\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)=\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])
,
so we are done.

5.

NF-esS
∙
. Then

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty u\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\},\mu}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},{\text{$\not$\!@}}}\hskip 8.5359pts\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=165.8465pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{NF-esS${}^{\bullet}$}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 46.14368pt\hbox{$\displaystyle u[x\backslash s]\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},\mu}$}}}}\end{array}

Since
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])}
implies

\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\},u)}

and
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)}
, then
by
i.h.
on
u
and
s
there exist
tight types
\mathbb{t}_{1}
and
\mathbb{t}_{2}
such that

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\},u)\vdash^{(0,0)}u:\mathbb{t}_{1}
and

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)\vdash^{(0,0)}s:\mathbb{t}_{2}
. Moreover,

\mathbb{t}_{2}=\,\mathbb{s}
since
s\in\mathsf{St}_{\mathcal{S}}
.
Notice that

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\},u)=\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u);x:\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S}\cup\{x\},u)(x)
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)(x)

is
\,\mathbb{s}
if
x\in\mathsf{rv}(u)
and
\bot
otherwise.
We build the following derivation:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 86.00429pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\text{(By {\it i.h.})}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=209.7919pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u);x:\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)(x)\vdash^{(0,0)}u:\mathbb{t}_{1}$}}}}\end{array}\hskip 8.5359pt\begin{array}[]{c}\enskip{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 29.62222pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\text{(By {\it i.h.})}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=97.02777pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)\vdash^{(0,0)}s:\,\mathbb{s}$}}}}\end{array}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=345.35558pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 76.39417pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)+\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)\vdash^{(0,0)}u[x\backslash s]:\mathbb{t}_{1}$}}}}\end{array}

where
\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)(x)\lhd\,\mathbb{s}
necessarily holds since

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},u)(x)
is
\bot
or
\,\mathbb{s}
.
Notice that

\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u)+\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},s)=\mathsf{TEnv}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S},u[x\backslash s])
,
so we are done.

∎

Lemma E.14 (Anti-substitution).

Let
\mathsf{inv}{(\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},t)}
.
Consider a subset
\mathcal{A}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{A}

and let
\mathcal{B}
be a set of variables disjoint from
\mathcal{A}
.
Suppose also that the following conditions hold:

(a)

t\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}t^{\prime}

(b)

\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}\vdash^{(m,e)}t^{\prime}:\mathcal{S}

(c)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{B}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?})

(d)

If
\mu=@

then either
\mathcal{S}=\,\mathbb{s}

or
\mathcal{S}
is a singleton, i.e., of the form
[\alpha]
.

(e)

v\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}_{0}\cup\mathcal{B}}

Then there exist
\Gamma_{t},\Gamma_{v},\alpha,m_{t},e_{t},m_{v},e_{v}

such that
\Gamma=\Gamma_{t}+\Gamma_{v}

and
m=m_{t}+m_{v}
and
e=e_{t}+e_{v}
;

\Gamma_{t};x:\mathcal{T}^{?}+[\alpha]\vdash^{(m_{t},e_{t}+1)}t:\mathcal{S}
;
and
\Gamma_{v}\vdash^{(m_{v},e_{v})}v:[\alpha]
.

Proof.

By induction on the derivation of
t\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},\mu}t^{\prime}
.

1.

sub
∙
.
Then the following conditions hold:

(a)

x\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\mathsf{sub}_{(x,v)},\mathcal{A}\cup\{x\},\mathcal{S},@}v
,
where
t=x,\mu=@
and
t^{\prime}=v

(b)

\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?}\vdash^{(m,e)}v:\mathcal{S}

(c)

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{B}\cup\{x\}}(\Gamma;x:\mathcal{T}^{?})

(d)

Since
\mu=@
, so either
\mathcal{S}=\,\mathbb{s}
or
\mathcal{S}
is of the form

.

(e)

v\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}_{0}\cup\mathcal{B}}

Recall that

by the grammar of rule names. We analyze by cases the form of
v
:

1.1

v=y
. Then
x\neq y
.
The judgement of condition (b) can be derived only by the rule

,
so it is of the form

,
with

.

\mathcal{T}^{?}
must be
\bot
.
And

,
since the judgement of condition (e) can only be derived by the rule

.
Then

by condition (c), as

\mathcal{A}_{0}\cup\mathcal{B}\cup\{x\}\subseteq\mathcal{A}\cup\mathcal{B}\cup\{x\}
.

\mathcal{S}=[\alpha]
for some type
\alpha
, and thus
{\tt ta}([\alpha])=1
necessarily holds.
Taking

\Gamma_{t}=\varnothing,\Gamma_{v}=y:[\alpha],\alpha,m_{t}=0,e_{t}=0,m_{v}=0,e_{v}=1

the following statements hold:

•

\Gamma=y:[\alpha]=\Gamma_{t}+\Gamma_{v}

and
m=0=m_{t}+m_{v}

and

•

, by rule
var

•

y:[\alpha]\vdash^{(0,1)}y:[\alpha]
, by condition (b).

v=\lambda y.\,u

The judgement of condition (b) can be derived only by the rule
abs
.
Moreover, we can derive the judgement

.

,
that is,
\mathcal{T}^{?}=\bot
.
Then the following derivation is for the judgement of condition (b):

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 8.19443pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Gamma;y:\mathcal{R}^{?}\vdash^{(m,e)}u:\mathcal{Q}$}}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=94.6055pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{abs}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}\lambda y.\,u:[\mathcal{R}^{?}\to\mathcal{Q}]$}}}}\end{array}

Taking

\Gamma_{t}=\varnothing,\Gamma_{v}=\Gamma,\alpha=\mathcal{R}^{?}\to\mathcal{Q},m_{t}=0,e_{t}=0,m_{v}=m,e_{v}=e

the following statements hold:

•

\Gamma=\Gamma_{t}+\Gamma_{v}

and
m=m_{t}+m_{v}

and
e=e_{t}+e_{v}

•

x:[\mathcal{R}^{?}\to\mathcal{Q}]\vdash^{(0,1)}x:[\mathcal{R}^{?}\to\mathcal{Q}]
,
by rule
var

•

\Gamma_{v}\vdash^{(0,1)}\lambda y.\,u:[\mathcal{R}^{?}\to\mathcal{Q}]
,
by condition (b).

, and

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}}}(\Sigma_{2};\Delta_{2})
,
since

Then
\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}

\mathtt{appropriate}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}}\cup\{x\}}(x:\mathcal{S}^{?})

(d’)

If
\mu=@

then either

or

(e’)

v\in\mathsf{HA}_{\mathcal{A}^{\mathtt{L}}}
,
by

Therefore by
Lemma

, we have that there exist

such that:

1’.

\Sigma_{1};\Delta_{1}=\Theta_{u}+\Theta_{v}
and

\Theta_{u};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}+[\alpha]\vdash^{(n_{u},f_{u}+1)}u:\mathcal{T}

3’.

\Theta_{v}\vdash^{(n_{v},f_{v})}v:[\alpha]

By statement 1’. we can write

\Theta_{u}
as
\Sigma_{11};\Delta_{11}
and

\Theta_{v}
as
\Sigma_{12};\Delta_{12}
with

By
\alpha-
conversion
the bound variables in
v\mathtt{L}
don’t occur free in

;
in particular
\mathtt{dom}(\mathtt{L})\cap\mathsf{fv}(u)=\varnothing
.
Since
\mathsf{dom}(\Delta)=\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2})=\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{1})\cup\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{2})

is a subset of

by

, then

, so in particular

\mathsf{dom}(\Delta_{2})\cap\mathsf{fv}(u)=\varnothing
.
We have

\mathsf{dom}(\Theta_{u};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}+[\alpha])\subseteq\mathsf{fv}(u)

by
Lemma

7.1
.
Therefore
\Delta_{11}=\varnothing
, with
\Delta_{1}=\Delta_{12}
.
We apply
Lemma

E.3
, merging the judgement of statement 3’. and
the judgement
\Sigma_{2};\Delta_{2}\vdash^{(n_{2},f_{2})}v:\mathcal{S}
,
so that we obtain

(\Sigma_{12};\Delta_{1})+(\Sigma_{2};\Delta_{2})\vdash^{(n_{v}+n_{2},f_{v}+f_{2})}v:[\alpha]+\mathcal{S}
.
We compose this result with the judgement from statement 1, by

, yielding

\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}+\Sigma_{12}+\Sigma_{2}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+n_{v}+n_{2},e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+f_{v}+f_{2})}v\mathtt{L}:[\alpha]+\mathcal{S}
.
We apply rule
es
:

\begin{array}[]{c}{\vbox{\hbox spread0.0pt{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\penalty\Sigma_{11};x:\mathcal{S}^{?}+[\alpha]\vdash^{(n_{u},f_{u}+1)}u:\mathcal{T}\hskip 8.5359pt\mathcal{S}^{?}\lhd\mathcal{S}\hskip 8.5359pt\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}+\Sigma_{12}+\Sigma_{2}\vdash^{(m^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+n_{v}+n_{2},e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+f_{v}+f_{2})}v\mathtt{L}:[\alpha]+\mathcal{S}$}\hskip 0.0pt}\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\vrule height=2.75002pt,depth=-2.24998pt,width=362.84834pt\hbox{\kern 3.00003pt$\textup{{{{es}}}}$}}}\hbox{\kern 47.84958pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\Sigma_{11}+\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}+\Sigma_{12}+\Sigma_{2}\vdash^{(n_{u}+m^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+n_{v}+n_{2},f_{u}+1+e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+f_{v}+f_{2})}u[x\backslash v\mathtt{L}]:\mathcal{T}$}}}}\end{array}

where

\Sigma_{11}+\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}+\Sigma_{12}+\Sigma_{2}=\Sigma_{1}+\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}+\Sigma_{2}=\Gamma_{u^{\prime}[x\backslash v]}+\Gamma_{\mathtt{L}}=\Gamma
.
Moreover, since
\rho=\mathsf{lsv}
, we have that

(m,e)=(n_{u}+m^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+n_{v}+n_{2},f_{u}+1+e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+f_{v}+f_{2})=(m^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+n_{1}+n_{2},f_{1}+f_{2}+e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+1)=(m^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+m^{\prime}_{u^{\prime}[x\backslash v]},e^{\prime}_{u^{\prime}[x\backslash v]}+e^{\prime}_{\mathtt{L}}+1)=(m^{\prime},e^{\prime}+1)
,
so we are done.

3.

The congruence cases are uninteresting and are omitted here.

∎

See
7.7

Proof.

By induction on
n
:

1.

n=0
.
Then
t\in\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}
,
and
m=e=0
by definition of
m
and
e
.
By
Proposition

7.5
,
there exists a tight type
\mathbb{t}
such that
\mathsf{TEnv}(\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),t)\vdash^{(0,0)}t:\mathbb{t}
,
with
\mathsf{TEnv}(\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),t)
a tight environment,
so we are done.

2.

n=n^{\prime}+1
.
Then
t\notin\mathsf{NF}^{\bullet}_{\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}
.
The reduction sequence is then of the form:

t\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho_{1},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}t^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho_{2},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}\ldots\xrightarrow{\raisebox{-2.2604pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{\footnotesize$\bullet$}}_{\rho_{n^{\prime}+1},\varnothing,\mathsf{fv}(t),{\text{$\not$\!@}}}u

where
n^{\prime}=m^{\prime}+e^{\prime}
and

m^{\prime}=\#\{i\ |\ 2\leq i\leq n^{\prime},\rho_{i}=\mathsf{db}\}\hskip 14.22636pte^{\prime}=\#\{i\ |\ 2\leq i\leq n^{\prime},\rho_{i}=\mathsf{lsv}\}

Since
\mathsf{fv}(t)=\mathsf{fv}(t^{\prime})
, since the reduction is non-erasing when
\rho_{1}\in\{\mathsf{db},\mathsf{lsv}\}
,
then we can apply
i.h.
, yielding that there exists a tight environment
\Gamma

and a tight type
\mathbb{t}
such that

\Gamma\vdash^{(m^{\prime},e^{\prime})}t^{\prime}:\mathbb{t}
.
By
Proposition 7.6 (Subject Expansion).
,
it holds that
\Gamma\vdash^{(m,e)}t:\mathbb{t}
, where
if
\rho=\mathsf{db}
then
(m,e)=(m^{\prime}+1,e^{\prime})
and
if
\rho=\mathsf{lsv}
then
(m,e)=(m^{\prime},e^{\prime}+1)
,
so we are done.

∎
